# Comparative analysis of the drought-responsive transcriptome in soybean lines contrasting for canopy wilting

Silvas J. Prince<sup>a</sup>,1, Trupti Joshi <sup>b</sup>,1, Raymond N. Mutava<sup>a</sup>,1, Naeem Syed<sup>c</sup>,1, Maldonado dos Santos Joao Vitor a, Gunvant Patil a, Li Songa, JiaoJiao Wang b, Li Lina, Wei Chena, J. Grover Shannona, Babu Valliyodana, Dong Xu<sup>b</sup>,c, Henry T. Nguyen<sup>a</sup>,<sup>∗</sup>

<sup>a</sup> National Center for Soybean Biotechnology and Division of Plant Sciences University of Missouri, Columbia, MO 65211, USA <sup>b</sup> Department of Computer Science, Informatics Institute, University of Missouri, Columbia, MO 65211, USA

<sup>c</sup> School of Human and Life Sciences, Canterbury Christ Church University, Canterbury CT1 1QU, United Kingdom

# a r t i c l e i n f o

Article history: Received 2 April 2015 Received in revised form 17 August 2015 Accepted 22 August 2015 Available online 28 August 2015

Keywords: Soybean Drought stress Aquaporin Canopy wilting RNA-sequencing

# a b s t r a c t

Drought stress causes significant yield losses in major oil seed crops, such as soybean [Glycine max (L.) Merr]. Few soybean lines have been identified as canopy-wilting tolerant; however, the molecular mechanism conferring tolerance is not fully understood. To understand the biological process, a whole genome transcriptome analysis was performed for leaftissues oftwo contrasting soybean lines: droughtsusceptible (DS) Pana and drought-tolerant (DT) PI 567690. A pairwise comparison of the DS and DT lines under drought and control conditions detected 1914 and 670 genes with a greater than two-fold change in expression under drought conditions. Pairwise treatment comparison and gene enrichment analysis on the DT line showed the down-regulation of genes associated with protein binding, hydrolase activity, carbohydrate/lipid metabolism, xyloglucan endo-transglycosylases associated with cell-wall, apoplast, and chlorophyll a/b binding proteins. On the other hand, genes that were associated with the biotic stress response, ion binding and transport, the oxido-reductive process and electron carrier activity were upregulated. Gene enrichment analysis detected UDP glucuronosyl transferase activity-encoding genes to be differentially expressed in PI 567690 under drought stress conditions. We found valuable SNPs variation in aquaporin genes of the DT line that are conserved in known slower canopy-wilting lines, this should facilitate marker-assisted selection in soybeans with improved drought tolerance.

## **1. Introduction**

Soybeans are a major oil seed crop worldwide and an indispensable dietary source of oil, protein, macronutrients, and minerals [\[1\].](#page-11-0) Drought stress has devastating effects on soybean yields [\[2\],](#page-11-0) and this situation is further exacerbated because most US soybean cultivars have a very narrow genetic base [\[3\].](#page-11-0) Recently soybean breeding programs have identified some exotic drought-tolerant lines with good yield potential from around the world [\[4\].](#page-11-0) Evaluating soybeans lines for drought tolerance is problematic and complex, as it is an unpredictable event under field conditions [\[5\].](#page-11-0) Canopy wilting is a visual symptom of soybean response to water deficit [\[6\]](#page-11-0) and has been used to identify drought-tolerant lines under field conditions [\[3,5\].](#page-11-0) This trait is polygenic in nature and

E-mail address: [nguyenhenry@missouri.edu](mailto:nguyenhenry@missouri.edu) (H.T. Nguyen).

[http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.plantsci.2015.08.017](dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.plantsci.2015.08.017) 0168-9452/© 2015 Elsevier Ireland Ltd. All rights reserved. could be affected by various attributes [\[7\],](#page-11-0) delaying the appearance of wilting symptoms. [\[8\].](#page-11-0) Ries et al. [\[9\]](#page-11-0) reported distinct combinations of key physiological traits, such as radiation-use efficiency and water-use efficiency, which result in soil moisture conservation among slow-wilting lines. The molecular mechanisms that are involved in drought/canopy-wilting tolerance remain unclear. Several studies have associated silver nitrate sensitive aquaporins with the canopy-wilting trait in soybean [\[10,11\],](#page-11-0) but none have identified specific aquaporins that are involved in this trait. Aquaporins are small integral membrane proteins that belong to a large family of water channel proteins and help in selective water transport across cellular membranes [\[12\].](#page-12-0) In addition to water transport, aquaporins are involved in the uptake of nutrients and carbon and nitrogen fixation [\[13\].](#page-12-0)

Sinclair et al. [\[14\]](#page-12-0) proposed that soybean genotypes with slower canopy wilting and the ability to sustain nitrogen fixation under drought (NFUD) yielded better under water-limited environments. Extensive research on the effects of soil drying and nitrogen fixation in soybean [\[15\]](#page-12-0) has shown that nitrogen fixation in soybean

declines under drought [\[16,17\],](#page-12-0) which ultimately affects seed yield [\[14,18\].](#page-12-0) Drought/canopy-wilting tolerance and nitrogen fixation efficiencyunderdrought are correlated withnodule size and weight in soybeans [\[19,20\].](#page-12-0) Most of the key canopy-wilting-tolerant soybean PI lines have a high nodule size and weight that enhance the nitrogen fixing ability under drying soil conditions [\[19,21,22\].](#page-12-0) Libault et al. [\[23\]](#page-12-0) reported eight fruit-weight-like genes that were associated with nodule organogenesis and that determine the nodule number and size in soybeans. However, the expression pattern and influence of these genes on the maintenance of nodules under drought stress have not been reported so far. Recently, canopywilting QTLs [\[8\]](#page-11-0) and nodule-related traits [\[24\]](#page-12-0) were mapped in the recombinant inbred line (RIL) that was developed between KS4895 and Jackson.

Recent advancements in sequencing technology and gene expression platforms have facilitated the discovery of genes that are associated with drought/dehydration stress in Arabidopsis [\[25\],](#page-12-0) rice [\[26\],](#page-12-0) and soybean [\[27\].](#page-12-0) Studies based on soybean Affymetrix gene chips provide only a limited picture of the whole genome transcriptome; however, next-generation sequencing technologies allow deeper and more robust transcriptome analyses [\[28\].](#page-12-0) RNAsequencing approaches provide precise information on differential gene expression in different organisms [\[29,30,31\].](#page-12-0) Transcriptome studies in soybean have been performed at the tissue level [\[28\],](#page-12-0) in response to bacteria [\[23\],](#page-12-0) and in the apical meristem tissues [\[32\].](#page-12-0) Transcriptome studies (with Affymetrix/RNA sequencing technology) aimed at understanding abiotic stresses in rice utilized contrasting lines for drought stress [\[26,33\],](#page-12-0) but most of the studies in soybean used a single genotype [\[27,34\].](#page-12-0) Transcriptome studies with contrasting lines will give comparative and detailed information on differentially expressed genes (DEGs) that could help identify useful genes that are involved in drought tolerance. A recent transcriptome study with contrasting soybean lines (Benning × PI416937) enabled the derivation of a deeper understanding of the drought response in soybean [\[35\].](#page-12-0) In addition, assessing genomic differences among soybean lines with the trait of interest (canopy wilting) will enable us to understand the molecular mechanism [\[36\]](#page-12-0) and identify genes for marker-assisted selection. Furthermore, integrating advances in -omics technologies (DNA and expression transcriptome) will enable us to hasten our understanding of complex traits and drought stress tolerance mechanisms in soybean [\[37,38\].](#page-12-0) In this study, a drought-tolerant and slow-wilting genotype, PI 567690, and a contrasting droughtsusceptible genotype, Pana, were used to elucidate the possible molecular mechanisms underlying these complex traits. The goal of this study was to identify the candidate genes that are involved in drought/canopy-wilting tolerance under drought conditions that could be potentially used in marker-assisted selection and in the development of drought-tolerant soybean lines.

## **2. Materials and methods**

#### 2.1. Plant materials and drought stress treatment

This experiment was conducted in 2012 under greenhouse conditions at the Division of Plant Sciences, University of Missouri, Columbia, Missouri, in a randomized, complete block design experiment with three replications. A mixture of soil and sand (2:1) was used in 26.5 L pots (the top and bottom diameters were 30 cm and 27 cm, respectively, and the height was 37 cm). The two genotypes, cultivar Pana, selection from Jack × Asgrow A3285, and a plant introduction (PI) 567690 from China, were selected as contrasting canopy-wilting lines based on a drought study that was conducted under greenhouse [\[39\]](#page-12-0) and field [\[22\]](#page-12-0) conditions. Further details on growing conditions in greenhouse and phenotypic variation in the physiological and biochemical traits of these two lines under drought stress conditions can be obtained from Mutava et al. [\[39\].](#page-12-0) At the V5 stage [\[40\],](#page-12-0) withdrawing water for 15 days imposed drought stress. After the stress treatment, the plants were in the R2 stage [\[40\].](#page-12-0) The relative water content based on the leaves that were collected from the fourth trifoliate leaf on the main stem at midday (11:00–13.30 h) was 48–59% under severe stress; these samples were collected in a 25 ml Falcon tube and flash frozen in liquid nitrogen for transcriptome analysis.

## 2.2. RNA isolation and qRT-PCR analysis

One hundred milligrams of leaf tissue was used for RNA extraction using the RNeasy Plant mini-kit (Qiagen, 214CA, USA) with on-Column DNA digestion performed using the RNase-Free DNase Set (Qiagen, CA, USA) according to the manufacturer's protocol. Total RNA (2 g) from each sample was reverse-transcribed to cDNA in a 20 -L reaction volume using the RNA to cDNA EcopryTM Premix (double-primed) cDNA Synthesis Kit (Clontech, CA, USA) as per the manufacturer's protocol. Quantitative RT-PCR (qRT-PCR) was performed using the cDNA product corresponding to 25 ng of total RNA in a 10 -L reaction volume using the Maxima SYBR Green/ROX qPCR Master Mix (2×) (Thermo, USA) on a detection system (ABI 7900HT [Life Technologies, Grand Island, New York, USA]). Seventeen genes were selected from comparative drought transcriptome data to validate the RNA sequencing expression data from leaf samples (3 biological and 2 technical replicates) using qRT-PCR analysis. The PCR conditions were 50◦C for 2 min and 95 ◦C for 10 min,followed by 40 cycles of 95 ◦C for 15 s and 60 ◦C for 1 min. To normalize the gene expression, Ubiquitin (Glyma20g27950) was selected as a housekeeping gene [\[41\]](#page-12-0) because it showed no expression change between genotypes and stress treatment. All of the gene-specific primers were designed using the Primer3 web inter<span id="page-2-0"></span>face [\[42\].](#page-12-0) The primer sequence information of the selected genes is given in Supplementary Table 1.

Supplementry material related to this article found, in the online version, at <http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.plantsci.2015.08.017>.

## 2.3. RNA seq analysis

The RNA of the DS and DT lines was multiplexed and sequenced for gene expression analysis. Three biological replicates of the DS line Pana (control and drought samples) and the DT line PI 567690 (control and drought samples) leaf sample RNA were multiplexed and sequenced on an Illumina Hi-Seq 2000 platform. The RNA concentration of each sample was approximately 200 ng/l with a quantity of 50 l. The initial base calling and quality filtering of the reads generated with the Illumina analysis pipeline (fastq format) were performed using a custom Perl script and the default parameters of the Illumina pipeline ([http://www.illunina.com\)](http://www.illunina.com). Additional filtering for poor-quality bases was performed using the FASTX-toolkit available in the FastQC software package [\(http://www.bioinformatics.babraham.](http://www.bioinformatics.babraham.ac.uk/projects/fastqc/) [ac.uk/projects/fastqc/\)](http://www.bioinformatics.babraham.ac.uk/projects/fastqc/).Good-quality mRNA-seq reads were aligned to the Glycine max reference genome (Gmax1.1 version) and Phytozome v9.0 gene model release using TopHat (version 1.4.1) [\[43,44\].](#page-12-0) The genome indexes for TopHat were built using the Bowtie-build command of Bowtie (version 0.12.7) with the reference genome file as input. TopHat was then run with default parameters and a reference GTF file using the –G option, and replicates of each condition/sample weremappedindependently to improve the alignment sensitivity and accuracy for further analysis. Only uniquely mapping reads were used in the analysis. The gene expression (FPKM) levels were estimated using Cufflinks software (version v2.1.1)[\[45\],](#page-12-0) while a differential gene expression analysis was performed using Cuffdiff (version v2.1.1) among the different sample comparisons. In this study, we selected differentially expressed genes that were selected with <sup>a</sup> significant p-value of less than <sup>5</sup> <sup>×</sup> <sup>10</sup>−<sup>5</sup> for further analysis. A pairwise comparison of treatments (drought and control condition) was performed in the DS and DT lines individually. In addition, a comparative study on the drought stress transcriptome was performed between the DS and DT lines. The analyzed RNA-seq gene expressions, as well as the differential gene expression results, are available for browsing in Soybean Knowledge Base (SoyKB)[\[46\]](#page-12-0) at <http://soykb.org> The datasets can be browsed using the differential expression analysis suite tools, which provide access to the gene lists, Venn diagrams, Volcano plots, and function, as well as to pathway analysis.

# 2.4. Co-expression gene models and functional enrichment analysis

The weighted gene co-expression network analysis (WGCNA) R package [\[47\]](#page-12-0) was used to analyze and perform pairwise correlation between a limited number (∼2000 genes) of most differentially expressed genes. Due to this limitation in the number of genes that could be analyzed, the fold change of selected genes and the number of genes differ for the WGCNA analysis as follows: Pana control and drought (>2-fold; 1487 genes), PI 567690 control and drought (>3-fold; 1512 genes), Pana-drought and PI 567690 drought (>5-fold; 396 genes) and Pana and PI 567690 drought (>2-fold; 609 genes) were considered for gene module construction. For gene co-expression analysis, similarity between the gene expression was defined by a similarity matrix with each gene corresponding to a node. The similarity matrix was transformed into an adjacency matrix. We used scale-free topology criteria to choose an adjacent function parameter pf signed R2 > 0.90 to derive the weighted network. The steps that were involved in this analysis and the parameters that were used are described in

**Fig. 1.** (A) Venn diagram showing overlap of differentially expressed genes in Pana (1728 genes) and PI 567690 (484 genes)underpairwise comparisonbetweencontrol and drought stress with 186 genes differentially expressed in both genotypes in drought condition (B) Pairwise comparison of drought and control conditions in specific to Pana (1577 genes) and PI 567690 (372 genes) to comparative drought transcriptome between Pana and PI 567690 (575 genes) and the common set of genes expressed between datasets detailed in the intersection of the Venn diagram.

detail by [\[48\].](#page-12-0) Modules, clusters of highly interconnected genes, were constructed, and network heat maps were constructed using the function Topological Overlap Matrix (TOM) plot with high coexpression inter-connectedness as indicated by progressively more saturated yellow and red colors [\[47\].](#page-12-0) To determine the biological meaning of the identified co-expression module, the up- and down-regulated genes within the modules were further analyzed for Pfam, Panther, KOG, and GO functional enrichment using the standalone version of the GeneMerge tool [\[49\]](#page-12-0) incorporated into SoyKB.

## 2.5. Gene-co expression analysis

The transcription factors that were identified in the DT lines were further analyzed using a gene co-expression analysis tool in the Genevestigator database [\[50\]](#page-12-0) with soybean Affymetrix data that were generated on 49 anatomical tissues in various experiments. We could not use the only available drought stress Affymetrix dataset, as this tool requires more samples to perform co-expression analysis. In this analysis, the genes that were coexpressed withthe genotype specific TFs were identified withmean person correlation co-efficient values calculated based on log2scaled expression data [\[50\].](#page-12-0) In the resulting circular co-expression plot,the target gene/Transcription factor of interestis in the center, and the correlated genes are displayed around at a distance corresponding to their correlation with the target gene; the closer they are to the center, the higher their correlation with the target gene across the selected soybean Affymetrix dataset. The co-expressed genes with a mean correlation value of greater than 0.7 were considered, and the mutually correlated genes were shown with connected lines.

## 2.6. DNA isolation, genome sequencing, and functional SNP identification

The DNA of known canopy-wilting-tolerant lines (PI 416937, PI 471938, PI 548657 (Jackson), andPI 567731) along withthe line that was selected in this study, PI 567690, were isolated and sequenced at a depth of 15× using Illumina 90 bp paired-end sequencing technology with insert sizes of approximately 500 bp. The data were processed after filtering low-quality reads and duplicate reads. The processed data were aligned to the William 82 Gmax v1.1 from Phytozome as the reference genome [\[51\].](#page-12-0) A phylogenetic tree was constructed with the lines that were used in the present study and other known slow-wilting lines using whole genome sequence data in MEGA 6 [\[52\]](#page-12-0) with the Neighbor joining option. SNPs and Indels were identified using an in-house-built pipeline with GATK v3.0 [\[53\]](#page-12-0) and were analyzed for possible synonymous/nonsynonymous SNP variation annotations using the SnpEFF [\[54\]](#page-12-0) and v9.0 gene models from Phytozome. Based on the established relationship of aquaporins to the canopy-wilting trait in soybean [\[10,11\],](#page-11-0) aquaporin genes in the soybean genome were selected [\[12\]](#page-12-0) for functional SNP identification that might affect the differential expression under drought conditions.

# **3. Results**

## 3.1. Identification of DEGs among treatments

Genome-wide transcriptome data of the genotypes (Pana and PI 567690) showed significantly different expression patterns under both drought and control conditions [\(Table](#page-1-0) 1). The droughtsusceptible (DS) genotype Pana had an average of 41,268 and 44,071 genes expressed under control and drought stress conditions, respectively. On the other hand, the drought-tolerant (DT) genotype (PI 567690) had 43,997 and 43,311 genes expressed under control and drought stress conditions, respectively. The DT line constitutively expressed more genes than did the DS line; thus, the DT line expressed fewer genes during stress imposition. Based on a pairwise comparison between drought stress and control conditions, 1914 and 670 genes were found to be differentially expressed [\(Table](#page-4-0) 2, [Fig.](#page-2-0) 1) in the DS and DT lines, respectively, and the genes were classified as shown in [Figs.](#page-4-0) 2 and 3. In this comparison, the DS line showed the up- and down-regulation of 1079 and 835 genes (Supplementary Table 2), and the DT line showed the up- and down- regulation of fewer genes (202 and 468 genes), as listed in Supplementary Table 3. The number of genes that were differentially expressed in the comparative drought transcriptome of the DS and DT lines is listed in [Table](#page-4-0) 2 and further classified as shown in [Fig.](#page-6-0) 4. A comparison of the DS (drought) and DT (control) treatments was performed to understand the constitutive expression of genes in the DT line that are only induced in the DS line after drought stress, and we detected 16,947 and 19,428 genes as up- and down-regulated in the DT line ([Table](#page-4-0) 2).

Supplementry material related to this article found, in the online version, at [http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.plantsci.2015.08.017.](http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.plantsci.2015.08.017)

### 3.2. DEGs encoding TFs in DS and DT genotypes

In drought stress, both the DS and DT lines differentially expressed 22 TFs in a pairwise comparison of the drought and control conditions (Supplementary Table 4). However, the number of genes encoding a particular TF family differed between them; for example, the DT line expressed more genes encoding the AUX-IAA-ARF transcription factor than did the DS line, Pana. In the DS line, a large number of transcripts of TFs, such as MYB/HD, AP2-EREBP and NAC, were differentially expressed (Supplementary Table 4). However, very few transcripts encoding these TFs were common among the DS and DT lines, with similar regulation patterns as shown in Supplementary Table 4. Other TFs, such as Aux-IAA-ARF, BZIP, C2C2 (zn) YABBY, and CSD, showed an opposite regulation pattern between the DS and DT lines dataset in the respective pairwise treatment comparisons s. In total, 17 and 1 HTH-ARAC-genotype-specific TF (Glyma04g04460) were identified as differentially expressed only in the DS and DT lines, respectively, in a pairwise comparison of treatments, and their regulation pattern is presented in [Table](#page-5-0) 3.

Supplementry material related to this article found, in the online version, at <http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.plantsci.2015.08.017>.

## 3.3. Analysis of DEGs between genotypes under drought conditions

A comparative analysis of DEGs between the two genotypes under drought condition was performed to identify DEGs that are found only in the drought-tolerant genotype. Under drought stress, 882 genes ([Table](#page-4-0) 2; [Fig.](#page-2-0) 1) were differentially expressed in the DT line: 268 up- and 614 down-regulated genes (Supplementary Table 5), classified as shown in [Fig.](#page-6-0) 4. The DT line differentially expressed 21 TF families [\(Table](#page-7-0) 4), including a genotype-specific FWL1 transcription factor (Glyma07g30800) that was identified in this comparative analysis alone. Some of the TFs that were identified in the DT line, such as AP2-EREBP (Glyma08g24420 and Glyma17g35860), bHLH (Glyma07g26910), TPR (Glyma08g22660), BZIP (Glyma06g43760), MYB-HD (Glyma04g04490 and Glyma10g36950), Auxin ARF (Glyma13g40310), and Homeodomain /HOMEOBOX (Glyma11g06940), were regulated in opposite directions in the DS line (shown within the parenthesis in the gene regulation column of [Table](#page-7-0) 4). Few transcripts encoding TFs, such as C2C2 (Zn) YABBY and C2C2 (Zn) CO-like, were regulated in the same direction in both the DS and DT lines [\(Table](#page-7-0) 4).

Supplementry material related to this article found, in the online version, at <http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.plantsci.2015.08.017>.

## 3.4. DEGs encoding TFs in the DT genotype

An analysis of the regulatory mechanism of drought-responsive genes in the DT line detected TFs such as HTH-ARAC and FWL1 (fruit weight 2.2-like 1) as differentially expressed, specifically in the DT line. The FWL1 (Glyma07g30800) was down-regulated only in the DT line only. We analyzed the co-expression of other target genes [\(Fig.](#page-6-0) 5) that might be regulated by this TF by using drought stress Affymetrix gene chip data available in soybean. This analysis identified this TF, Glyma07g30800, as showing a positive Pearson correlation coefficient value of <0.767 with late-embryogenesis-abundant proteins (Glyma19g38600 and Glyma19g39920), an uncharacterized protein (Glyma07g39540), Plastocyanin-like domain (Glyma03g26060), and carbonic anhydrase (Glyma02g37710).

#### 3.5. Validation of RNA-sequencing expression by QRT-PCR

To validate the RNA-sequencing-based gene expression level that correlated with the number of reads that were obtained, qPCR was performed on 17 randomly selected genes with different expression levels in the DS and DT lines and functional assignments. The QRT-PCR results of the tested differentially expressed genes show the same trend that was observed in RNA sequencing data even though the magnitude of change is different ([Fig.](#page-8-0) 6).

### 3.6. Transcriptome variation among genotypes

The gene expression analysis between the control treatments of DS and DT detected 1211 genes and 1542 as down- and upregulated, respectively. This set of constitutively expressed genes might be involved in general metabolic and development pathways of soybean. The set of genes overlapping between the comparisons (DT and DS control and DS and DT drought) represents genes that are induced by drought in the DS line and are already expressed in the DT line in the absence of stress. Most of the overlapped

up- and down-regulated transcripts identified are listed in Supplementary Table 6. Most of the up-regulated transcripts were encoded for FE (II) oxygenase family, ABC transporter, cytochrome p450, sugar transporters, Leucine-rich repeats, UDP glucuronosyl transferase activity and key TFs, such as AP2 domain, AUX/IAA, and F-box. The PI 567690 also constitutively downregulated four transcripts of NAC TF (Glyma06g11970, Glyma12g22880, Glyma12g35000, and Glyma13g35550), followed by other TFs, such as PLATZ (Glyma01g44430), WRKY (Glyma13g38630), AP2

(Glyma20g34570), CCAAT (Glyma15g12570), Homeodomain (Glyma07g05800) and MYB/HD like (Glyma04g33720) compared to Pana under drought conditions. A common mechanism of tolerant plants to overcome stress conditions is to constitutively express genes that help cope with stress and that are normally induced in susceptible plants, giving the tolerant genotype a "head start" that can account for the tolerant phenotype. A comparative WGCNA analysis of DS (drought) and DT (control) also identified five different gene modules (Supplementary Fig.

The common drought response genes that were up-regulated encode for oxido-reductases, transferases, DNA binding and cellular transport activities. The down-regulation of many transcripts that encode metabolic processes, DNA binding, oxido-reductases activity, iron binding, and transcription regulation were observed. ## 3.7. Drought molecular signatures identified

A WGCNA analysis was performed to determine the similarity of genes that were expressed across conditions (control and drought) and classify key genes that govern the drought response. The gene coexpression modules that were identified from the DS line between control and drought stress are illustrative of the results that can be obtained using a WGCNA analysis with coexpression data. Expression values from a total of 1487 genes were analyzed. Six modules were identified (Supplementary Fig. 2), and the number of genes per module ranged from 63 (red module) to 599 (turquoise module). An enrichment analysis was performed to

#### 3.8. Differential expression of membrane intrinsic proteins

The expression levels of 104 membrane intrinsic proteins/aquaporins genes in soybean were mined (Supplementary Table 11) to associate them with the canopy-wilting trait. However, only 18 genes that were expressed in these contrasting lines and their regulation pattern are shown in [Fig.](#page-9-0) 7. The pairwise comparison of the DS line under drought and control conditions showed the up-regulation of plasma-membrane-intrinsic proteins (PIP) (Glyma01g42950, Glyma05g37730, Glyma07g02060, Glyma08g01860, and Glyma18g42630) and a tonoplast intrinsic protein (TIP) (Glyma07g02060), whereas the other three down-regulated aquaporins are PIPs (Glyma12g29510 and Glyma13g40100) and a TIP (Glyma04g08831). A similar comparison in the DT line revealed eleven aquaporin genes (PIPs: Glyma04g00450, Glyma05g37730, and Glyma08g01860; TIPs: Glyma04g08831, Glyma07g02060, Glyma08g21730, Glyma15g02090 and Glyma19g37000; and aquaporins associated with glycerol

#### 3.9. Sequence level variation for aquaporins among canopy-wilting lines

Atthe whole genome level,the slow-wilting lines that wereused in the present study and other known slow-wilting sources are genetically diverse (Fig. 8). However, single nucleotide polymorphism (SNP) variation was found only in the up- (Glyma01g42950, Glyma05g37730, Glyma08g01860 and Glyma18g42630) and downregulated (Glyma13g40100) PIP aquaporins ofthe DS line compared to the DT line ([Table](#page-10-0) 7). The aquaporin PIP, Glyma08g01860, in the DT line showed a SNP variation at six positions downstream of this gene [\(Table](#page-10-0) 8). Interestingly, all six of the SNPs were also found among other known slow-wilting lines (PI 416937, PI 471938, PI 567731, and Jackson). In addition, Glyma13g40100 was downregulated in the DS line associated with glycerol transport and showed a synonymous coding SNP mutation ([Table](#page-10-0) 8)in the DT line

**Fig. 8.** Phylogenetic analysis among known slow wilting soybean lines with whole genome sequence data generated at 15x sequencing depth using MEGA 6 software

and other slow-wilting lines (PI 471938 and PI 567731). Another PIP, Glyma18g42630, which was down-regulated in the DT line, had a non-synonymous SNP in this gene that changed the amino acid from serine to asparagine ([Table](#page-10-0) 8). Thus, the association between these SNPs and the canopy-wilting phenotype needs to be established in the future.

# **4. Discussion**

## 4.1. Drought-stress-specific transcriptome

Under drought stress conditions, the DS genotype differentially regulated more genes compared to the DT line [\(Table](#page-4-0) 2). In addition, the DT lines also down-regulated the genes that were associated with carbohydrate and lipid metabolism, which might be associated with slowing the exhaust of energy reserves, allowing survival during drought stress conditions. This result is evident by the increased level of fructose and glucose with the increase in the total sugar concentration in this DT line under drought stress conditions

<sup>a</sup> Soybean plant introduction (PIs) reported to show slower canopy wilting response in drought stress condition.

[\[39\].](#page-12-0) In contrast, DS upregulates these genes that limit crop survival and productivity under stress environments [\[55\].](#page-12-0) However, the transcriptome data and WGCNA analysis showed that the DT line expressed similar genes under both drought and control conditions. This result shows that the DT line constitutively expresses genes that help cope with stress, whereas they are only induced in the DS line after drought stress. This constitutive expression of genes in the DT line includes FE(II) oxygenase family, ABC transporter, cytochrome p450, sugar transporters, Leucine-rich repeats and UDP glucuronosyl transferase activity and key TFs, such as AP2 domain, AUX/IAA, and F-box (Supplementary Table 6), making it genetically well adapted and potentially needing to regulate fewer genes for its adaptive process under water-limited conditions compared to the DS line. Similar constitutive expression of genes was reported in a slow-wilting line, PI 416937, at a genotype level comparison with Benning, a drought-susceptible line [\[35\].](#page-12-0) The pairwise comparison of treatments in the DS line showed the down-regulation ([Table](#page-9-0) 6) of genes associated with photosynthesis, electron-transport system and the up-regulation of genes associated with iron binding and transport, oxidation-reduction process, and cell wall and related processes (Supplementary Table 8). Differences in antioxidant activity under salinity and drought stress have been reported among tolerant and susceptible lines of wheat [\[56\]](#page-12-0) and bean [\[57\],](#page-12-0) respectively. In addition, the down-regulation of these photosynthesis related gene ontologies has been reported in soybean as an early and linear response to drought stress [\[35\].](#page-12-0) However, none of the photosynthesis-related genes were downregulated in the DT line transcriptome datasets, suggesting that the photosynthetic process in the DT line might have acclimated as drought stress occurred. Mutava et al. [\[39\]](#page-12-0) observed a higher photosynthetic rate in this DT line compared to the DS line under drought conditions. Similar photosynthetic acclimation has also been observed in plants under mild or moderate drought stress [\[58\].](#page-12-0)

In the comparative transcriptome analysis, the DT line down-regulated many transcripts that were associated with the oxidation-reduction process and apoptosis process and upregulated only genes encoding UDP glucuronosyl transferase activity [\(Table](#page-8-0) 5). In soybean, the down regulation of oxidation reduction genes has been reported as an early response to drought conditions [\[35\].](#page-12-0) Other studies in crops such as rice and tomato, the up-regulation of similar functional genes and other genes associated with sugar metabolism and detoxification [\[59,60,61\]](#page-12-0) and osmotic adjustment [\[62\],](#page-12-0) respectively, were reported under drought conditions. In rice, these genes are involved in stress protection [\[63\]](#page-12-0) and in the transfer of sugar to various acceptor molecules, such as flavonoids, under drought [\[33\]](#page-12-0) and salt stress conditions [\[64\].](#page-13-0) The responses that were observed in the DT line, such as the constitutive expression of stress-responsive genes and the early activation of adaptive mechanisms to maintain whole plant level energy homeostasis, might have resulted in a higher drought yield index under drought conditions in this genotype [\[22\]](#page-12-0) as a result of enhanced plant performance under harsh growing environments [\[55\].](#page-12-0) The tolerant lines adapt and survive stress conditions by the rapid perception of stress signals, which leads to the modification of growth and development patterns [\[65\].](#page-13-0) The relationship between the impact of energy metabolism on plant productivity and stress tolerance is well documented [\[66\].](#page-13-0)

The WGCNA analysis identified the up-regulation of iron binding- and iron transport-related genes in both the DS (Supplementary Table 6) and DT (Supplementary Table 8) lines, but the DS line up-regulated two specific iron-binding proteins (Glyma05g33280 and Glyma07g12741), which are involved in respiratory burst, a vital defense mechanism against nitrogen-fixing bacterial infection in legumes [\[67\].](#page-13-0) Our results seems to speculate that these defensive proteins might be involved in the facilitation of the nodulation bacteria to be active under drought conditions, thereby increasing the nodule size and weight in the DT line, as reported by Pathan et al. [\[22\],](#page-12-0) but their expression in root tissues is unknown. Abdel-Haleem et al. [\[68\]](#page-13-0) identified QTLs for the canopywilting trait that co-localized with many QTLs for iron efficiency in soybean. Many of the chlorophyll a/b binding proteins (Supple<span id="page-11-0"></span>mentary Table 7) were down-regulated in the DT line. However, the down-regulation of these proteins had no effect on the photosynthetic activity under drought stress conditions with higher stomatal conductance, despite higher abscisic acid levels in leaf tissue in the DT line [\[39\].](#page-12-0) Xu et al. [\[69\]](#page-13-0) also reported the reduction of the responsiveness of stomatal movement to ABA in Arabidopsis with the down-regulation of chlorophyll-binding protein.

#### 4.2. Genotype-specific transcriptome

The DT line significantly expressed more genes encoding AUX-IAA-ARF TF during drought stress conditions (Supplementary Table 4). A similar up-regulation of this TF in response to abiotic stress conditions has been reported in rice [\[33\]](#page-12-0) and soybean [\[34\].](#page-12-0) The comparative transcriptome revealed the up-regulation of an AUX-IAA-ARF TF, Glyma05g35640, and the down regulation of Glyma06g07130 and Glyma19g34370 ([Table](#page-7-0) 4). Some studies have reported the involvement of AUX-IAA-ARF TFs in delayed leaf senescence in Arabidopsis [\[70,71\].](#page-13-0) Ha et al. [\[72\]](#page-13-0) also reported the possible role of AUX-IAA-ARF TFs in the leaf senescence of soybean. Thus, the role of these auxin TFs needs to be tested to establish their role in canopy wilting in soybean. The fruit weight like 1 (FWL1), a PLAC8 protein homologous to the tomato FW2.2 gene, was downregulated in the DT line leaf tissue. Although Libault et al. [\[23\]](#page-12-0) reported that FWL1 was localized in the plasma membrane and involved in nodule development, more research is needed to illuminate its roles in the nodule size and weight increase under drought conditions. The late-embryogenesis-abundant (LEA) proteins were co-expressed with FWL1 TF in this study. The LEA protein has been reported as up-regulated in drought and heat stress in Brassica [\[73\]](#page-13-0) and as conferring drought tolerance in chickpea [\[74\]](#page-13-0) and rice [\[75\].](#page-13-0) However, the role of LEA genes in drought tolerance or canopy wilting in soybean is not known and therefore should be studied further. In tomato, the FW2.2 gene co-regulates a YABBY TF, which is involved in the cell cycle and in the determination of the floral organ number to influence the tomato fruit size [\[76\].](#page-13-0) Similarly, a key C2C2 (Zn) YABBY TF, Glyma05g04260, was also expressed in the comparative transcriptome along with the FWL TF in the DT line. The interaction between these TFs needs to be elucidated under drought stress conditions.

#### 4.3. Role of aquaporins in drought/canopy-wilting tolerance

Most of the aquaporins (PIPs) were down-regulated under drought stress conditions in the DT line to potentially avoid excessive water loss and to maintain the leaf hydraulic content. The down-regulationof aquaporinshelps plants to acclimate to drought stress conditions. Aquaporins also maintain water uptake and its movement at the leaf level and therefore may play a role in the slower canopy-wilting response in the DT line [\[22\].](#page-12-0) A similar downregulation of aquaporins was observed in Arabidopsis in response to drought stress to stop the further production of aquaporins to minimize water flow through cell membranes and maintain leaf turgor [\[77\].](#page-13-0) Most of the aquaporins that were up-regulated in the DS line were plasma-membrane-intrinsic proteins (PIP) that were involved in water transport. The up-regulation of aquaporins has been reported to avoid pressure build up in xylem vessels that cause embolism in plants as a result of transpirational water loss [\[13\].](#page-12-0) Sinclair et al. [\[78\]](#page-13-0) suggested that water conservation as a drought-tolerance mechanism results in a slower-wilting phenotype in soybean PI 416937. Further evaluation of this genotype revealed the absence of silver-sensitive aquaporins [10]. Among plant aquaporins, TIPs are known to store and transport urea in plants [\[79\];](#page-13-0) therefore, the down-regulation of TIP in the DT line may result in maintaining urea equilibrium by storing excess urea within the vacuole under drought stress conditions. TIP2 homologs are involved in loading urea and trapping it in the vacuole in Arabidopsis [\[80\].](#page-13-0) Zhang et al. [\[12\]](#page-12-0) identified only one PIP aquaporin each on chromosome 5 and 8 (Glyma05g37730 andGlyma08g01860) that co-localizes with the reported canopy-wilting QTLs in soybean [8,68].Astudy by Mutava et al.[\[39\]](#page-12-0) showed a lower reduction in the transpiration rate in the DT line under drought-stress conditions compared to the DS line. Similar responses in slow canopy-wilting soybean lines have been associated with a silver-sensitive aquaporin [\[11\].](#page-12-0) The SNP variation in aquaporins associated with water transport(Glyma07g02060 andGlyma08g01860) and glyceroltransport (Glyma13g40100) were common in the known slow-wilting soybean lines and in the DT line that was used in this study [\(Tables](#page-10-0) 7,8). These sequence-level variations in aquaporin genes could be effectively used as functional molecular markers for complex traits, such as canopy wilting or drought tolerance in soybean. These findings will help understand the function of genes and their regulation underlying this complex trait and phenotypic response under drought stress conditions.

#### **5. Conclusion**

We found that aquaporins (water and glycerol transporters), chlorophyll a/b-binding proteins and TFs (FWL and YABBY) are the potential candidates to improve canopy-wilting tolerance and to ensure productivity under drought conditions in soybean. Further studies on elucidating the role of these TFs (FWL and YABBY) and their interacting partners will enable us to enhance productivity under drought stress conditions. Functional markers that were developed with aquaporins with non-synonymous SNPs should facilitate the marker-assisted selection of the canopy-wilting trait in soybean.
